N_{1}
N_{1}
N_{x}

(b)

\mathfrak{T}_{x}
infinite

Figure 7
.
Possibilities for
\mathfrak{T}_{x}

For the induction step, assume that we have already defined a subset
U_{i}\subseteq\mathfrak{L}_{2}(N,i)
.
Let
H_{i}=\{x\in U_{i}\mid x\in N_{2},\mathcal{L}_{2}(x)=i+1\}
be the set of leaves in
\mathfrak{T}[U_{i}]
that are in
N_{2}
.
For each
x\in H_{i}
, we consider the subtree that starts at
x
until we
reach the next node from
N_{2}
, including the node itself.
Everything below such a node will be cut.
To be precise, we regard the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{x}=(N_{x},E_{x},L_{x})=\mathfrak{T}[\mathfrak{L}_{2}(xE^{*},i+1)]
. Here,
xE^{*}
is the set of all nodes that
are reachable from
x
by arbitrary many steps.

First, we show that
|\mathfrak{T}_{x}|=1
.
For every direct successor
y
of
x
, the subtree
\mathfrak{T}_{y}=\mathfrak{T}_{x}[yE_{x}^{*}]
of
\mathfrak{T}_{x}

that starts at
y
does not contain any inner nodes from
N_{2}
.
Hence, we have
|\mathfrak{T}_{y}|=1
by the prerequisite of the lemma, and hence

|\mathfrak{T}_{x}|=\sum_{y\in xE}p_{y}\cdot|\mathfrak{T}_{y}|=\sum_{y\in xE}p_{y}\cdot 1=\sum_{y\in xE}p_{y}=1.

For the construction of
U_{i+1}
, we have the same cases as before, see
Figure

7
.
Either
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
is finite or
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
is infinite.
Let
Z_{x}
be the set of nodes that we want to add to our node set
U_{i+1}
from the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
.
In the first case we can add all the nodes again and set
Z_{x}=N_{x}
.
In the second case, we once again cut the tree at a specific depth once the probability for leaves is high enough.
Let
\operatorname{d}_{x}(z)
be the depth of the node
z
in the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
.
Moreover, let
D_{x}(k)=\{x\in N_{x}\mid\operatorname{d}_{x}(z)\leq k\}
be the set of nodes in
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
that have a depth of at most
k
.
Since
|\mathfrak{T}_{x}|=1
and
|\_|
is monotonic w.r.t. the depth of the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
, we can find an
N_{x}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}},d_{x}(y)\leq N_{x}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\geq 1-\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{i+1}\cdot\frac{1}{|H_{i}|}

We include all nodes from
D_{x}(N_{x})
in
U_{i+1}
and delete every other node of
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
.
In other words, we cut the tree after depth
N_{x}
.
We now know that this cut may increase the probability of leaves by at most
\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{i+1}\cdot\frac{1}{|H_{i}|}
.
Therefore, we set
Z_{x}=D_{x}(N_{x})
.

We do this for each
x\in H_{i}
and in the end, we set

U_{i+1}=U_{i}\cup\bigcup_{x\in H}Z_{x}
.

It is straightforward to see that
\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}U_{k}
satisfies the conditions of
Section

A.2
, as we only cut after certain nodes in our construction.
Hence,
\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}U_{k}
is non-empty and weakly connected, and for each
of its nodes, it either contains no or all successors.
Furthermore,
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{T}[\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}U_{k}]
is a subtree which
does not contain an infinite path of pure
N_{1}
nodes as we cut every such
path after a finite depth.

It remains to prove that
|\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}|\leq|\mathfrak{T}|+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{n}
holds.
During the
i
-th iteration of the construction, we may increase the value of
|\mathfrak{T}|
by the sum of all probabilities corresponding to the new leaves resulting from the cuts.
As we cut at most
|H_{i}|
trees in the
i
-th iteration and for each such tree, we added at most a total probability of
\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{i+1}\cdot\frac{1}{|H_{i}|}
for the new leaves, the value of
|\mathfrak{T}|
might increase by

|H_{i}|\cdot\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{i+1}\cdot\frac{1}{|H_{i}|}=\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{i+1}

in the
i
-th iteration, and hence in total, we then obtain

|\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}|\leq|\mathfrak{T}|+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)^{n},

as desired (see (
3
)).

With the cutting lemma, we can now prove the following lemma regarding the parallel execution of
rewrite sequences that are
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
.

Lemma 54

(Parallel Execution Lemma for
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a PTRS and
s\in\mathbb{S}
.
Furthermore, let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\in\mathcal{T}
be terms such that for every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}_{i}
that starts with
(1:q_{i})

for some
1\leq i\leq n
we have
|\mathfrak{T}_{i}|=1
.
Then, every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
for some symbol
\mathsf{c}
,
where we do not rewrite at the root position,
converges with probability
1
.

Proof A.9
.

By
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$s\>\lnot\varepsilon$}}}{{\smash{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
we denote the restriction of
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
that does not perform any rewrite steps at the root position.
We prove that every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$s\>\lnot\varepsilon$}}}{{\smash{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST which starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
for some symbol
\mathsf{c}

converges with probability
1
.
Note that if we rewrite a term
q_{i}
to
\{p_{1}:q_{i,1},\ldots,p_{k}:q_{i,k}\}
, then we obtain a distribution
\{p_{1}:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{i,1},\ldots,q_{n}),\ldots,p_{k}:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{i,k},\ldots,q_{n})\}
.
Now, the terms
q_{j}
with
j\neq i
occur multiple times in this distribution, and we may use different rules to rewrite them.
Hence, the order in which we rewrite the different
q_{i}
matters and cannot be
chosen arbitrarily (as seen in Counterex.
24
).
Therefore, the proof is much more complex than in the non-probabilistic setting for termination.
(This is not the case for leftmost-innermost rewriting, where an easier induction step
than
the following one would also be possible.)
Let
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
be the restriction of
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$s\>\lnot\varepsilon$}}}{{\smash{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
where we can only
rewrite on or
below a position
1\leq i\leq e
.
Note that
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle n}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}\;=\;\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$s\>\lnot\varepsilon$}}}{{\smash{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
By induction on
e
, we prove
that every
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST which starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
converges
with probability 1.

In the base case we have
e=1
and only allow rewrite steps on or below position
1
.
Obviously, since every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:q_{1})
converges with probability
1
by our assumption,
so does every
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle 1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
.

In the induction step, we assume that the statement holds for
e-1<n
,
i.e., every
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST which starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))

converges with probability
1
.
Now consider an arbitrary
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
.
Assume for a contradiction that it converges with a probability
<1
.
The rest of the induction step has the following structure:

1)

We first use the cutting lemma (
Lemma

53
) to get rid of infinite paths that only
use rewrite steps on or below position
e
, resulting in an
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST
tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
,
which uses infinitely many
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-steps in each infinite path.

2)

Then, for each finite height
H\in\mathbb{N}
we split the tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}
,
which consists of the first
H
layers of the tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
, into multiple
(finitely many)
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs
of height at most
H

that all start with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))

Furthermore, we show that for each
H\in\mathbb{N}
, at least one of these

\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs converges with low enough probability.

3)

We create an infinite, finitely-branching tree whose nodes
are labeled with RSTs. More precisely, its
nodes at depth
H\in\mathbb{N}
are labeled with

\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs with low enough probability.

4)

Finally, we use König’s Lemma to obtain an infinite path in this tree, which corresponds to an infinite

\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
and converges with probability
<1
,
which is our desired contradiction.

1) Use the cutting lemma

We can partition the inner
nodes of our RST
\mathfrak{T}
into the sets

\bullet

N_{1}:=\{x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}\mid
the rewrite step at
x
is on or below position
e\}

\bullet

N_{2}:=N\setminus N_{1}=\{x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}\mid
the rewrite step at
x

is on or below position
k
with
1\leq k<e\}

We know that every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:q_{e})
converges with probability
1
by assumption.
Hence, also every subtree of
\mathfrak{T}
that only contains nodes from
N_{1}
as inner
nodes
converges with probability
1
.
Thus, we can apply the cutting lemma (
Lemma

53
) and obtain a subtree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{T}

that converges with probability
<1
and contains infinitely many nodes of
N_{2}
in each infinite path.

2) Split the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
for every
H\in\mathbb{N}

Let
H\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}
be the finite tree consisting of the first
H
layers of
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
,
where the subterm on position
e
remains the same in every node and is equal to the
subterm on
position
e
in the root of
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
converges with probability
<1
, there exists an
0<\alpha\leq 1

such that
|\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}|=\lim_{H\to\infty}\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}\land\operatorname{d}(x)\leq H}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}<1-\alpha
,
and hence
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}\land\operatorname{d}(x)\leq H}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}=\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}<1-\alpha
for every
H\in\mathbb{N}
.
Note that
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}
is not a valid
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST, as we now have steps where no term changes, i.e., where we would have performed a rewrite step below position
e
.

From
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}
we generate a set
\mathbb{T}_{H}
of
pairs

(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})
where

p_{\mathfrak{t}}\in(0,1]
is a probability and

\mathfrak{t}
is an
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST of height at most
H

(i.e., we do not perform any rewrite steps on or below position
e
anymore).
The set
\mathbb{T}_{H}
contains all
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs
\mathfrak{t}
resulting from

\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}
when skipping the nodes of
N_{1}
(where the rewrite step is on or below the
position
e
).
Instead, one uses one of its child nodes. Moreover,

p_{\mathfrak{t}}
is the probability for choosing the RST
\mathfrak{t}
.
We will give more intuition on

\mathbb{T}_{H}
below.
Since
a rewrite step on or below the position
e
may create several children (one for each term
in the support of the multi-distribution on the right-hand side of the applied rewrite
rule), the set
\mathbb{T}_{H}
may contain several pairs.

\mathbb{T}_{H}
will satisfy the following two properties:

(Prop-1)

We have
\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}=1
. This means that the sum of the probabilities for all trees in
\mathbb{T}_{H}
sum up to one.

(Prop-2)

For all
x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}\cup N_{2}

we have
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}=\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}
. As mentioned above,
p_{\mathfrak{t}}
represents the
probability that the RST
\mathfrak{t}
is chosen and
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}
is the
probability of the node
x
in the RST
\mathfrak{t}
. Whenever
x
is not a
node of
\mathfrak{t}
(i.e.,
x\notin N^{\mathfrak{t}}
), then we define

p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}=0
.
This means that the probability for a leaf
x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}
or
an inner node
x\in N_{2}
in our cut tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}
is equal to the sum
over all trees
\mathfrak{t}
that contain
x
, where we multiply the probability

p_{\mathfrak{t}}
of the tree
\mathfrak{t}
by the probability of node
x
in
\mathfrak{t}
.

Assume now that for every
(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}
we have
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\geq 1-\alpha
.
Then, with the two properties

(Prop-1)
and
(Prop-2)
we obtain
the following:

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}^{\prime}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}^{\prime}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by \ref{itm:const-prop-2})}}&=&\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}^{\prime}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}^{\prime}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}^{\prime}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by assumption)}}&\geq&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot(1-\alpha)\\
&=&(1-\alpha)\cdot\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by \ref{itm:const-prop-1})}}&=&(1-\alpha)\cdot 1\\
&=&1-\alpha\end{array}

(4)

which is a contradiction.
Hence, for each
H\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a
(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}
such that

\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}<1-\alpha

(5)

We will use this in Step 3) and 4) to generate our infinite
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST that converges with probability
<1
.
But first we have to define the set
\mathbb{T}_{H}
that satisfies

(Prop-1)
and
(Prop-2)
.

Idea of
\mathbb{T}_{H}

The idea of the split can be seen in
Figures

8
and
9
.
There, the probabilities of the nodes are indicated by the
small
numbers

and the probabilities for the edges are indicated by the
big numbers
.
Whenever we encounter a node from
N_{1}
with successors
y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}
,
meaning that we rewrite on or below position
e
,
then we split the tree into
m
different trees,
since the terms below positions
1
to
e-1
did not change,
and we may use different rules on the different resulting terms afterwards.

1
e

.

\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{2}
with a step on or below position
e
in the left node

.

\mathbb{T}_{2}=\{(\tfrac{1}{4},\mathfrak{t}_{1}),(\tfrac{3}{4},\mathfrak{t}_{2})\}

Constructing
\mathbb{T}_{H}

To construct
\mathbb{T}_{H}
, we
repeatedly remove all nodes of
N_{1}
from the tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}

and create a set
M
inductively that satisfies the following properties.

(Ind-1)

\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M}p_{\mathfrak{t}}=1
.

(Ind-2)

For all
(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M
and
all inner nodes
x\in N^{\mathfrak{t}}
with successors
y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}
in
\mathfrak{t}
,
the edge relation either represents a valid rewrite step on or
below a position
1\leq j\leq e-1
, or the subterms
on or below the positions
1\leq j\leq e-1

remain the same,
but the probabilities of the successors still sum up to the probability of node
x
,
i.e.,
\sum_{j=1}^{k}p_{y_{j}}^{\mathfrak{t}}=p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}
.

(Ind-3)

For all
x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}\cup N_{2}

we have
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}=\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M}p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}
.

We stop the construction once every
\mathfrak{t}\in M
is a valid
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!}
-RST,
i.e., once we have
removed all nodes
where the terms do not change as we would have performed a rewrite step on or below position
e
.
In the end, we result in a set
M
that satisfies
(Ind-1)
,
(Ind-2)
, and
(Ind-3)
,
and
\mathfrak{t}
is a valid
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!}
-RST for every
(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M
.
Then
M
is our desired set, and we define
\mathbb{T}_{H}=M
.
Moreover, in the end,
Item

(Prop-1)
and
Item

(Prop-2)
follow from

(Ind-1)
and

(Ind-3)
.

We start with
M=\{(1,\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H})\}
.
Here, clearly all three properties

(Ind-1)
-
(Ind-3)
are satisfied.
Now, assume that there is still a pair
(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\in M
such that
\mathfrak{l}

contains a node
v\in N_{1}
that is not a leaf in
\mathfrak{l}
.
We will now split
\mathfrak{l}
into multiple trees that do not contain
v

anymore but move directly to one of its children, as illustrated in
Figure

10
.

First, assume that
v
is not the root of
\mathfrak{l}
.
Let
vE^{\mathfrak{l}}=\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}\}
be the direct successors of
v
in
\mathfrak{l}

and let
z
be the predecessor of
v
in
\mathfrak{l}
.
Instead of one tree
\mathfrak{l}
with the edges
(z,v),(v,w_{1}),\ldots,(v,w_{m})
,
we split the tree into
m
different trees
\mathfrak{l}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{l}_{m}

such that for every
1\leq h\leq m
, the tree
\mathfrak{l}_{h}
contains a direct edge from
z
to
w_{h}
.
In addition to that, the unreachable nodes are removed,
and we also have to adjust the probabilities of all (not necessarily direct) successors of
w_{h}

(including
w_{h}
itself) in
\mathfrak{l}_{h}
.

z
v
w_{1}
\ldots
w_{m}
\leadsto
z
v
w_{1}
\ldots
w_{m}
\ldots
z
v
w_{1}
\ldots
w_{m}

Figure 10
.
Skipping inner node
v\in N_{2}
to create
m
different trees

More precisely, we set
\mathfrak{l}_{h}=(N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}},E^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}},L^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}})
, with

\displaystyle N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}

\displaystyle=(N^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus v(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*})\cup w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}

\displaystyle E^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}

\displaystyle=(E^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus(v(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}\times v(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}))\cup\{(z,w_{h})\}\cup(E^{\mathfrak{l}}\cap(w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}\times w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}))

Furthermore, let
p_{h}=\tfrac{p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}}{p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}}
.
Then, the labeling is defined by

L^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}(x)=\begin{cases}(\frac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}},t_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}})&\text{ if }x\in w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}\\
(p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}},t_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}})&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Note that

\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}p_{h}=\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}\tfrac{p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}}{p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}}=\tfrac{1}{p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}}\cdot\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{itm:const-induction-2}}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}}\cdot p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}=1

(6)

If
v
is the root of
\mathfrak{l}
, then we use the same construction, but we have no predecessor
z
of
v
and directly start with the node
w_{h}
as the new root.
Hence, we have to use the node set
N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}
and edge relation
E^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=(E\cap(w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}\times w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}))
and the rest stays the same.

In the end, we set

M^{\prime}=M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}\cup\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}

This construction is exactly what we did in

Fig.

8
and

Fig.

9
for the only
node where we would have rewritten on or below position
e
.
It remains to prove that

(Ind-1)
,
(Ind-2)
, and
(Ind-3)

are still satisfied for
M^{\prime}
.

(Ind-1)
:

We have

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M^{\prime}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}\,p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{h}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}\,p_{h}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\text{(by \eqref{p_h sum is one})}}&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot 1\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\\
&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{=}}&1\end{array}

(Ind-2)
:

Let
1\leq h\leq m
.
We only split nodes whenever we would have
rewritten on or below position
e
.
Hence, we only have to prove that in this case the probabilities of the successors for a node add up to the probability for the node itself with our new labeling.
We constructed
\mathfrak{l}_{h}
by skipping the node
v
and directly moving from
z
to
w_{h}
(or starting with
w_{h}
if
v
was the root node).
Hence,
(N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}},E^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}})
is still a finitely branching tree.
Let
x\in N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
with
xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}\neq\varnothing
.
If
x\in w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{t}})^{*}
, then
xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=xE^{\mathfrak{l}}
and thus

\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}\tfrac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\tfrac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\tfrac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\tfrac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}

If
v
was not the root and
x=z
, then
zE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=(zE^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus\{v\})\cup\{w_{h}\}
and thus

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in zE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in(zE^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus\{v\})\cup\{w_{h}\}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\sum_{y\in(zE^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus\{v\})}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}+p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\sum_{y\in(zE^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus\{v\})}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}+\tfrac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in(zE^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus\{v\})}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}+\tfrac{p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}}{p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}}\cdot p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\sum_{y\in(zE^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus\{v\})}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}+p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\sum_{y\in zE^{\mathfrak{l}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{z}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{z}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}

Otherwise, we have
x\in N^{\mathfrak{l}}\setminus(v(E^{\mathfrak{t}})^{*}\cup\{z\})
.
This means
p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}
for all
y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
and
xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=xE^{\mathfrak{l}}
, and thus

\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\sum_{y\in xE^{\mathfrak{l}}}p_{y}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}

For the last property
(
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
), note that if
v
is not the root in
\mathfrak{l}
, then the
root and its labeling did not change, so that we have

p_{\mathfrak{r}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=p_{\mathfrak{r}^{\mathfrak{l}}}^{\mathfrak{l}}=1
, where

\mathfrak{r}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\mathfrak{r}^{\mathfrak{l}}
is the root of
\mathfrak{l}_{h}
and
\mathfrak{l}
.
If
v
was the root, then
w_{h}
is the new root with

p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\frac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}=\tfrac{p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}}{p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}}\cdot p_{w_{h}}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{v}^{\mathfrak{l}}=1

(Ind-3)
:

Let
x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}\cup N_{2}
.
If we have
x\not\in N^{\mathfrak{l}}
, then also
x\not\in N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
for all
1\leq h\leq m
and thus

\begin{array}[]{cl}&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M^{\prime}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}\cup\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot 0\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot 0\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{=}}&p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}\end{array}

If we have
x\in N^{\mathfrak{l}}
and
x\not\in v(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}
, then
x\in N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
for all
1\leq h\leq m
and
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}=p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
. Hence

\begin{array}[]{cl}&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M^{\prime}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}\cup\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},{\mathfrak{l}}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}\,p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{h}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}\,p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{h}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot\sum_{1\leq h\leq m}\,p_{h}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot 1\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{=}}&p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}\end{array}

Otherwise we have
x\in N^{\mathfrak{l}}
and
x\in v(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}
.
This means that we have
x\in N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}
and
x\in w_{h}(E^{\mathfrak{l}})^{*}
for some
1\leq h\leq m
and
x\not\in N^{\mathfrak{l}_{h^{\prime}}}
for all
h^{\prime}\neq h
.
Furthermore, we have
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}=\frac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}
, and hence

\begin{array}[]{cl}&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M^{\prime}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}},\mathfrak{l})\}\cup\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\{(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{1}),\ldots,(p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{m},\mathfrak{l}_{m})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{h}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}_{h}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{h}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{h}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M\setminus\{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}+p_{\mathfrak{l}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{l}}\\
=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in M}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{=}}&p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{H}}\end{array}

3) Create the finitely-branching infinite tree of RSTs

Next, we create a tree
\mathfrak{F}
whose nodes are labeled with RSTs. More precisely, its
nodes
at depth
H

represent
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs
\mathfrak{t}\in\mathbb{T}_{H}

that converge with a small enough probability,
i.e.,
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}<1-\alpha
.
The root of
\mathfrak{F}
is labeled with the subtree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{0}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
that only
consists of its root.

\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{0}
is a finite subtree and

\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{0}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}_{0}}=0\leq 1-\alpha
,
since the root of
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
cannot be a leaf in
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
.

Let
H\in\mathbb{N}
with
H>1
.
For the tree
\mathfrak{F}
, we have a node at depth
H
for every tree
\mathfrak{t}\in\mathbb{T}_{H}

such that
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{T}<1-\alpha
.
We draw an edge from a node
X
at depth
H-1
to the node
Y
at depth
H

if the corresponding RSTs in the labels are the same, or
if the RST for node
Y
is an extension of the RST for node
X

(i.e., we evaluate the leaves in the RST of
X
further to obtain
Y
).

Each
\mathbb{T}_{H}
for every
H\in\mathbb{N}
is finite, so
\mathfrak{F}
is finitely branching.
Furthermore, there is a node in each layer of the tree
\mathfrak{F}
, since for every
H\in\mathbb{N}
,
we can find an
(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}

such that
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}<1-\alpha
,
see (
5
).

4) Use König’s Lemma

Now we have an infinite tree
\mathfrak{F}
that is finitely branching,
which means that the tree has an infinite path by König’s Lemma.
This path represents an
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST that does not converge with probability
1
,
which is our desired contradiction.
To see this, let
\mathfrak{t}_{1},\mathfrak{t}_{2},\ldots
be the finite
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs in
the labels
of the nodes in the infinite path in
\mathfrak{F}
, where
\mathfrak{t}_{i}
is a prefix of

\mathfrak{t}_{i+1}
for all
i\geq 1
. Hence, we can
define the tree
\mathfrak{t}_{\lim}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathfrak{t}_{i}
.
Furthermore, there exists no infinite path in
\mathfrak{F}
such that the sequence
\mathfrak{t}_{1},\mathfrak{t}_{2},\ldots

eventually stays the same finite tree forever, due to the fact that there are no infinite paths in
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}

that eventually only contain nodes from
N_{1}
(due to the application of the cutting
lemma to create
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
).
Finally, we have
|\mathfrak{t}_{\lim}|=\lim_{i\to\infty}\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}_{i}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}_{i}}<1-\alpha
since
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}_{i}}\land x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}_{i}}<1-\alpha
holds for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

We also obtain a corresponding parallel execution lemma for
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
.
Note that the parallel execution lemma
does not hold for
\mathtt{PAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
, see
Section

3.3
.

Lemma 55

(Parallel Execution Lemma for
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a PTRS and
s\in\mathbb{S}
.
Furthermore, let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\in\mathcal{T}
be terms and
C_{1},\ldots,C_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
constants
such that for every
1\leq i\leq n
and every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}_{i}
that starts with
(1:q_{i})

we have
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T}_{i})\leq C_{i}
.
Then, every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
for some symbol
\mathsf{c}
,
where we do not use rewrite steps at the root position,
has a finite expected derivation length,
which is bounded by
\sum_{i=0}^{n}C_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, i.e.,
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T})\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}C_{i}
.

Proof A.10
.

We use the same induction on
e
as in the proof for the parallel execution lemma for
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}

(
Lemma

54
).
Note that we now need to prove an upper bound (the expected derivation length is smaller than
\sum_{i=0}^{n}C_{i}
),
while for
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
we had to prove a lower bound
(probability of convergence is (at least)
1
).
Therefore, we do not use the cutting lemma and the proof is a bit different in the induction step,
while the base case is again trivial.

In the induction step, we assume that the statement holds for
e-1
,
i.e., there exists a bound
C_{e-1}^{\prime}=\sum_{i=0}^{e-1}C_{i}\in\mathbb{R}

such that for every
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))

we have
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T})\leq C^{\prime}_{e-1}
.
Now consider an arbitrary
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
.
We prove that its expected derivation length is bounded by
C^{\prime}_{e-1}+C_{e}
.

Let
H\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
be the tree consisting of the first
H
layers
of
\mathfrak{T}
.
We partition the inner nodes of
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
into
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
, analogous to the
proof of the parallel execution lemma for
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}

(
Lemma

54
). As in that proof, for each
H\in\mathbb{N}

we split the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{H}

into multiple (finitely many)
\mathrel{\xhookrightarrow{{}_{\scriptstyle e-1}}\!\!_{\mathcal{P}}}
-RSTs
of height at most
H

that all start with

(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
. This again leads to a set of pairs
\mathbb{T}_{H}
such that:

(Prop-1)

\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}=1

(Prop-2)

For all
x\in N_{2}
we have

p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}=\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}

using the same notation as before.
Furthermore, we can use the same construction to create a set
\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}
containing
(finitely many) pairs of probabilities and
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RSTs that start with
(1:q_{e})
of height at most
H
,
by simply switching when to split the tree and when to perform the rewrite step.
To be precise, we split the tree if we encounter a rewrite step on or below a position
1\leq j\leq e-1
,
and perform the rewrite step if it is on or below position
e
.
Again, we get

(Prop-1-e)

\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}=1

(Prop-2-e)

For all
x\in N_{1}
we have

p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}=\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}

Now we can bound the expected runtime after height
H
by
C^{\prime}_{e-1}+C_{e}
for every
H\in\mathbb{N}
,
as we have

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T}_{H})\\
&=&\sum_{x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(since $N_{1}\uplus N_{2}=N^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}$)}}&=&\sum_{x\in N_{1}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\;+\;\sum_{x\in N_{2}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by \ref{itm:const-prop-2-SAST-e} and \ref{itm:const-prop-2-SAST})}}&=&\sum_{x\in N_{1}}\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&&+\;\sum_{x\in N_{2}}\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(as in \eqref{eq:array-AST-parallel})}}&=&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot\sum_{x\in N^{\mathfrak{t}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&&+\;\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot\sum_{x\in N^{\mathfrak{t}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{t}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by assumption and induction hypothesis)}}&\leq&\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot C^{\prime}_{e-1}\\
&&+\;\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot C_{e}\\
&=&C^{\prime}_{e-1}\cdot\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{e}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
&&+\;C_{n}\cdot\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by \ref{itm:const-prop-1-SAST-e} and \ref{itm:const-prop-1-SAST})}}&=&C^{\prime}_{e-1}\cdot 1+C_{e}\cdot 1\\
&=&C^{\prime}_{e-1}+C_{e}\end{array}

This gives us
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T})=\lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T}_{H})\leq C^{\prime}_{e-1}+C_{e}
,
as desired.

With the parallel execution lemmas for
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
and
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}

we can finally prove our modularity results. We start with
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
.

See
39

Proof A.11
.

The direction “
\Longrightarrow
” is trivial and thus, we only prove
“
\Longleftarrow
”. So let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\cup\mathcal{P}^{(2)}
, where
both
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}}
and
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}}
hold.

By
Lemma

49
, for
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
it suffices to regard only rewrite sequences that start
with multi-distributions of the form
\{1:t\}
.
Thus, we show by
structural induction on the term structure that for every

t\in\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{V})
, all

\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightrightarrows}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-rewrite sequences starting with
\{1:t\}

converge with probability 1.

If
t\in\mathcal{V}
, then
t
is in normal form.
If
t
is a constant, then w.l.o.g. let
t\in\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
.
Since we have
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}}
,
\{1:t\}
cannot start an infinite
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightrightarrows}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-rewrite sequence that converges with probability
<1
.

Now we regard the induction step, and consider the case where

t=f(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n})
.
By the induction hypothesis, every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}

that starts with
(1:q_{i})
for some
1\leq i\leq k
converges with probability
1
.
Let
\mathfrak{T}
be a fully evaluated
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
that starts with
(1:f(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
.
We prove that for every
0<\delta<1
we can find an
M\in\mathbb{N}

such that
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}},d(x)\leq N}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}>1-\delta
,
which means that
|\mathfrak{T}|=\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}},d(x)\leq k}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}=1
.
(Recall that for proving
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
, it suffices to consider only fully
evaluated RSTs, see
Corollary

50
.)

Let
0<\delta<1
.
By the induction hypothesis and the
parallel execution lemma (
Lemma

54
),
the maximal subtree
\mathfrak{T}_{\lnot\varepsilon}
of
\mathfrak{T}
that starts
with
\mathfrak{T}
’s root node and
only performs rewrite steps at non-root positions converges with probability

1
.
Since
|\mathfrak{T}_{\lnot\varepsilon}|=1
,
there exists a depth
H
such that
\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{\lnot\varepsilon}},d(x)\leq H}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\geq\beta
with
\beta=\sqrt{1-\delta}
.
Let
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
be the tree resulting from
cutting the tree
\mathfrak{T}
at depth
H
,
and let
Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}
be the set of leaves in
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
that were already leaves in
\mathfrak{T}_{\lnot\varepsilon}
.
So we have
\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}=\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{\lnot\varepsilon}},d(x)\leq H}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\geq\beta
.
For each leaf
x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}
, every proper subterm of
t_{x}
is in normal form
w.r.t.
\mathcal{P}
.
This is due to the fact that we use an innermost rewrite strategy and the RST
\mathfrak{T}
is
fully evaluated.
Let us look at the induced subtree
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
of
\mathfrak{T}
that starts at
x
(i.e.,
\mathfrak{T}_{x}=\mathfrak{T}[xE^{*}]
).
W.l.o.g., let the root symbol
f
of
t_{x}
be from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{T}^{(1)}
result from
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
by labeling the root with

t_{x}^{\prime}
,
where
t_{x}^{\prime}
results from
t_{x}
by
replacing all its maximal (i.e., topmost)
subterms with root symbols from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}

by fresh variables (using the same variable for the same subterm).
Obviously, since both PTRSs have disjoint signatures
and all proper subterms of
t_{x}
are in normal form,
we can still apply the same rules as in
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
,
such that
\mathfrak{T}^{(1)}
is
an
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
-RST with
|\mathfrak{T}^{(1)}|=|\mathfrak{T}_{x}|
.
Since every
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
-RST converges with probability
1
,
we obtain
1=|\mathfrak{T}^{(1)}|=|\mathfrak{T}_{x}|
.

For any node
y
of
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
,
let
\operatorname{d}_{x}(y)
be the depth of the node
y
in the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
.
Moreover, let
D_{x}(k)=\{y\in N^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\mid\operatorname{d}_{x}(y)\leq k\}
be the set of nodes in
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
that have a depth of at most
k
.
Since
|\mathfrak{T}_{x}|=1
and

|\_|
increases weakly monotonically
with the depth of the tree, we can find an
M_{x}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
|\mathfrak{T}_{x}[D_{x}(M_{x})]|>\beta
.

Note that
Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}
is finite and thus
M_{\max}=\max\{M_{x}\mid x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\}
exists.
Now, for
N=H+M_{\max}
we finally have

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}},d^{\mathfrak{T}}(x)\leq N}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\\
=&\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}},d^{\mathfrak{T}}(x)\leq H+M_{\max}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\\
\geq&\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\land\operatorname{d}_{x}(y)\leq M_{\max}}\,p_{y}^{\mathfrak{T}}\\
=&\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\land\operatorname{d}_{x}(y)\leq M_{\max}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\cdot p_{y}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\\
=&\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\cdot\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\land\operatorname{d}_{x}(y)\leq M_{\max}}\,p_{y}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\\
\geq&\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\cdot\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\land\operatorname{d}_{x}(y)\leq M_{x}}\,p_{y}^{\mathfrak{T}_{x}}\\
>&\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\cdot\beta\\
=&\beta\cdot\sum_{x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}}\\
\geq&\beta\cdot\beta\\
=&1-\delta\\
\end{array}

The first inequality holds since every leaf in
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
with a depth of at most
M_{\max}
(in
\mathfrak{T}_{x}
) for some
x\in Z^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}

must also be a leaf in
\mathfrak{T}
with a depth of at most
H+M_{\max}
, since
x
is at a depth of at most
H
.

Before we can prove modularity for disjoint unions w.r.t.
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
,
we have to give some more definitions regarding the disjoint union abstraction.
In addition to
Section

7.1
, we also want to label the function symbols in the abstraction,
to indicate from which position the function symbol originated from in the original
term. For a term
t
, let

\mathrm{Pos}_{\mathcal{V}}(t)
be the set of all its variable positions and
let
\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(t)
be the set of all those positions of
t
where

t
has function symbols instead of variables.

{defi}

[Labeled Disjoint Union Abstraction]
Let
\mathcal{P}^{(1)},\mathcal{P}^{(2)}
be PTRSs with
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}\cap\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}=\varnothing
.
For any
d\in\{1,2\}
,
t\in\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}\cup\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}},\mathcal{V})
,
and position
\pi\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(t)
,

\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi}(t)
and
\operatorname{Abs}_{d}(t)
are multisets of terms
from
\mathcal{T}(L(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(d)}}),\mathcal{V})
, which are defined as follows. Here, we
use the signature
L(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(d)}})=\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(d)}}\times\mathrm{Pos}(t)
.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi}(y)&=\{x\}\text{, if }y\in\mathcal{V}\text{, where
$x$ is always a new fresh variable }\\
\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\{f^{\pi}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{k})\mid q_{1}\in\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi.1}(t_{1}),\ldots,q_{k}\in\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi.k}(t_{k})\}\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(d)}}\\
\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\{x\}\cup\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi.1}(t_{1})\cup\ldots\cup\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi.k}(t_{k})\text{, otherwise, where
$x$ is always a new variable }\end{array}

So
\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\pi}(t)
is always a linear term, i.e., it never contains multiple occurrences of the
same variable.

For any function
\varphi:X\to X
with
X\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
let
\sigma_{\varphi}
be the substitution that replaces
every variable
x\in X
by
\varphi(x)\in X

and leaves all other variables unchanged, i.e.,
\sigma_{\varphi}(x)=\varphi(x)
if
x\in X
and
\sigma_{X}(x)=x
otherwise.
Then we define

\operatorname{Abs}_{d}(t)=\{\sigma_{\varphi}(q)\mid q\in\operatorname{A}_{d}^{\varepsilon}(t),\varphi:\mathcal{V}(q)\to\mathcal{V}(q)\}

and
\operatorname{Abs}(t)=\operatorname{Abs}_{1}(t)\cup\operatorname{Abs}_{2}(t)
.
The
(labeled) disjoint union abstraction
of
t
is the multiset
\operatorname{Abs}_{1}(t)\cup\operatorname{Abs}_{2}(t)
.

{exa}

Consider the PTRS
\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(1)}
with the rules
\mathsf{a}\to\{1:\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b})\}
,
\mathsf{b}\to\{1:\mathsf{0}\}
, and
\mathsf{h}(y,y)\to\{1:y\}
, the PTRS

\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(2)}
with the rule
\mathsf{g}(y)\to\{1:\mathsf{c}\}
,
and the term
\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x))
.
We obtain
14
14

14

To be precise,

\operatorname{A}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x)))
contains two additional terms

\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{a}^{1.1},z^{\prime})
and

\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(y,z^{\prime})
since
\operatorname{A}^{2}_{1}(\mathsf{g}(x))=\{z,z^{\prime}\}
. However, to
ease readability, we disregarded them here.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\operatorname{A}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x)))&=\{\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{a}^{1.1},z),\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)\}\\
\operatorname{Abs}_{1}(\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x)))&=\{\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{a}^{1.1},z),\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(z,y),\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(y,z),\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(z,z),\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(y,y)\}\\
\operatorname{A}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x)))&=\{x^{\prime},\mathsf{g}^{1}(y),\mathsf{g}^{2}(z)\}\\
\operatorname{Abs}_{2}(\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x)))&=\{x^{\prime},\mathsf{g}^{1}(y),\mathsf{g}^{2}(z)\}\end{array}

Furthermore, we will define
which terms
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
“cover” which of the
nodes from
N^{\mathfrak{T}}

for an arbitrary
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\cup\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
starting with
(1:t)
.
The idea is that every step in a rewrite sequence starting with
t

(corresponding to an inner
node of
\mathfrak{T}
)
can also be performed when starting with a suitable
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
.
For this, we first define the notion of an
origin graph
.
For every rewrite sequence starting with
t
, the graph indicates which subterm
“originates” from which subterm of
t
.
Moreover, every function symbol in the origin graph is labeled by the position of the
corresponding symbol on the right-hand side of the rule that created it.

{defi}

[Origin Graph]
Let
\mathcal{P}
be a PTRS
and let
\mathfrak{T}
be an
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST.
The
origin graph
for
\mathfrak{T}
is a labeled graph with the nodes
(x,\pi)

for all
x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}
and all
\pi\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(t_{x})
,
where the edges and labels are defined as follows:
For the root
\mathfrak{r}
of
\mathfrak{T}
we label the node
(\mathfrak{r},\pi)
by
\varepsilon
.
For
x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}
,
let the rewrite step
t_{x}\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}\{p_{1}:t_{y_{1}},\ldots,p_{k}:t_{y_{k}}\}
be performed using the rule

\ell\to\{p_{1}:r_{y_{1}},\ldots,p_{k}:r_{y_{k}}\}
, the position
\tau
, and the substitution
\sigma
,
i.e.,
t_{x}|_{\tau}=\ell\sigma
and
t_{y_{j}}=t_{x}[r_{j}\sigma]_{\tau}
for all

1\leq j\leq k
.
Let
\pi\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(t_{x})
.

(a):

If
\pi<\tau
or
\pi\bot\tau
(i.e.,
\pi
is above or parallel to
\tau
),
then there is an edge from
(x,\pi)
to
(y_{j},\pi)
.
If
(x,\pi)
was labeled by
\gamma
, then
(y_{j},\pi)
is labeled by
\gamma
as well.

(b):

For
\pi=\tau
,
there is an edge from
(x,\pi)
to
(y_{j},\pi.\alpha)
for all

\alpha\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(r_{j})
.
If
(x,\pi)
was labeled by
\gamma
, then
(y_{j},\pi.\alpha)
is labeled by
\gamma.\alpha
.

(c):

For every variable position

\alpha_{\ell}\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\mathcal{V}}(\ell)
, for all
positions
\alpha_{r_{j}}\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\mathcal{V}}(r_{j})
with

r_{j}|_{\alpha_{r_{j}}}=\ell|_{\alpha_{\ell}}
,
and for all
\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
with

\alpha_{\ell}.\beta\in\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(\ell\sigma)
, there is an edge from

(x,\tau.\alpha_{\ell}.\beta)
to
(y_{j},\tau.\alpha_{r_{j}}.\beta)
.

(d):

For all other positions
\pi\in\mathrm{Pos}(t_{x})
,
i.e., the positions that are inside the redex
\ell\sigma

but neither at the root of
\ell
nor inside the substitution
\sigma
,
there is no outgoing edge from the node
(x,\pi)
.

{exa}

Reconsider the PTRSs
\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(1)}

with the rules
\mathsf{a}\to\{1:\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b})\}
,
\mathsf{b}\to\{1:\mathsf{0}\}
, and
\mathsf{h}(y,y)\to\{1:y\}
,
the PTRS

with the rule
\mathsf{g}(y)\to\{1:\mathsf{c}\}
,
and the term
\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x))
from

.
Furthermore, consider the following RST, where we omitted the (trivial) probabilities,
and numbered each rewrite step.

\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\underline{\mathsf{a}}),\mathsf{g}(x))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{f}(\underline{\mathsf{b}})),\mathsf{g}(x))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathsf{h}(\underline{\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{0}))},\mathsf{g}(x))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{c},\underline{\mathsf{g}(x)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{c},\mathsf{c})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathsf{c}

This RST yields the following

\textstyle{(}

,
