\textstyle{\mathsf{c}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varepsilon}}}

Note that the root of every subterm that is not in normal form
is reachable from exactly one node
(\mathfrak{r},\pi)
.
Only nodes
(x,\tau)
where
t_{x}|_{\tau}
is in normal form may have multiple incoming transitions.

Our goal is to split up the RST that started with
(1:t)
into RSTs that start with

(1:q)
for
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
. The reason is that these terms
q
only contain symbols
from either
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}
or
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}
and hence, there is a bound on
the expected derivation length of all these RSTs.

The labels in the origin graph and the labels in the terms
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
can now be
used to construct the new RSTs that start with
(1:q)
for
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)

from the original RST that starts with
(1:t)
. Then every rewrite step in the original
RST corresponds to at least one step in one of these new RSTs.
Let us illustrate this with our running example.

{exa}

From the
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}_{14}}
-RST in
Section

A.3
we obtain
the following
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(1)}}
-RSTs
and
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(2)}}
-RSTs
15
15

15

In addition to the RST starting with

\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(z,z)
we also obtain the corresponding RST starting with

\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(y,y)
, but omitted it here for readability.

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(\underline{\mathsf{a}^{1.1}},z)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1^{\prime})}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}(\underline{\mathsf{b}}),z)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2^{\prime})}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{0}),z)\\
\underline{\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(z,z)}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5^{\prime})}}{{\longrightarrow}}&z&&\\
\underline{\mathsf{g}^{1}(z)}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3^{\prime})}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathsf{c}\\
\underline{\mathsf{g}^{2}(z)}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4^{\prime})}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathsf{c}\end{array}

where the labels are ignored for the rewrite steps, but they are used
to determine which start term from
\operatorname{Abs}(t)
to use for which step.
The rewrite step
(i)
in the original RST corresponds to the rewrite step
(i^{\prime})
in
our new RSTs.
In the original RST, one first performs rewrite steps for the symbols
\mathsf{a}
and
\mathsf{b}

from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(1)}}
, then one rewrites the symbol
\mathsf{g}
from

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(2)}}
above, and finally one rewrites the top symbol
\mathsf{h}
from

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(1)}}
. In contrast, these rewrite steps are now separated such that in
each of the above RSTs, one either only rewrites symbols from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(1)}}
or
only symbols from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}_{14}^{(2)}}
.

Let us explain how to detect that the rewrite step
(2)
that is performed at position
\pi=1.1.1

in
\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b})),\mathsf{g}(x))
in the original RST
should be applied at position
1.1
in
\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b}),z)
for rewrite step
(2^{\prime})
.
We consider the (unique) predecessor
(t_{\mathfrak{r}},1.1)

(i.e.,
\mathsf{a}^{\varepsilon}
)
of
(x_{2},1.1.1)
(i.e.,
\mathsf{b}^{1}
), where
\mathfrak{r}
is the root of the original RST
and
x_{2}
denotes the second node of the original RST containing the term

\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b})),\mathsf{g}(x))

This indicates that the rewrite step
(2)
in the original RST corresponds to a rewrite
step in a new RST that starts with
(1:q)

for a term
q
containing

\operatorname{root}(t_{\mathfrak{r}}|_{1.1})^{1.1}=\operatorname{root}(\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{a}),\mathsf{g}(x))|_{1.1})^{1.1}=\mathsf{a}^{1.1}
. Hence, we have to consider the
new RST starting with
\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(\underline{\mathsf{a}^{1.1}},z)
.

To find the actual rewrite step that corresponds to
(2)

in this new RST, we determine
the position of the symbol

\mathsf{a}^{1.1}

within
\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{a}^{1.1},x)
, which is
\gamma=1
,
and the label of
(x_{2},1.1.1)
(i.e.,
\mathsf{b}^{1}
), which is
\chi=1
. This
indicates that in the new RST we need to rewrite at position
\gamma.\chi=1.1
.

Now, we define this covering formally in order to
determine which term
q
from
\operatorname{Abs}(t)
to use for the current rewrite step.
More precisely, for any
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
we define the
abstraction cover

AC_{q}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{T}}
which
contains all inner nodes
of the original RST whose rewrite step can be simulated by a rewrite step in the new RST for
q
.
Moreover, for every
node
x
, we define the term
\psi_{x}(q)
which we would obtain instead of
t_{x}
if we
had started the RST with
q
instead of
t
.
Thus, if there is an edge from the node
x
to the node
y
in the original RST
\mathfrak{T}
, then

\psi_{x}(q)
rewrites to
\psi_{y}(q)
or they are equal. Therefore, for the root

\mathfrak{r}
of
\mathfrak{T}
, the term
\psi_{\mathfrak{r}}(q)
rewrites (in zero or more steps) to

\psi_{x}(q)
for every node of
\mathfrak{T}
.

{defi}

[Abstraction Cover]
Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\cup\mathcal{P}^{(2)}
be a PTRS
with
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}\cap\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}=\varnothing

and let
\mathfrak{T}
be an
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:t)
.
We define the
abstraction cover
with
\bigcup_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}AC_{q}=N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}
recursively,
where we additionally define a term

\psi_{x}(q)
for each node
x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}

that corresponds
to
t_{x}
in the RST starting with
(1:q)
.
So to reach
\psi_{x}(q)
from
q
, one performs the same
rewrite steps as in the path from the root to
x
in
\mathfrak{T}
whenever possible,
where the appropriate position of the new rewrite step is
indicated by the labels of the symbols in
q
and the labels in the origin graph.
For instance, in
Section

A.3
for
q=\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{a},z)
we have
\psi_{x_{2}}(q)=\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b}),z)

and
\psi_{x_{i}}(q)=\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{0}),z)
for all
i\in\{3,4,5,6\}
(we remove the
labels in
\psi_{x}(q)
).

For the root node
\mathfrak{r}
of
\mathfrak{T}
, we initially set
\psi_{\mathfrak{r}}(q)=q
.

Now consider the rewrite step at an arbitrary node
x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}
,
which uses the rule
\ell\to\{\ldots,p_{j}:r_{j},\ldots\}
at position
\pi
in
t_{x}
with substitution
\delta
.
Hence, we have
t_{y_{j}}=t_{x}[r_{j}\delta]_{\pi}
.
Let the origin graph of
\mathfrak{T}
contain a path from
(\mathfrak{r},\tau)
to
(x,\pi)
for
some position
\tau
and let

Q\subseteq\operatorname{Abs}(t)
be the set of
all terms from
\operatorname{Abs}(t)
that contain a
function
symbol labeled with
\tau
.
Moreover, let
(x,\pi)
be labeled with position
\chi
in the origin graph of
\mathfrak{T}
.
Then
we add
x
to
AC_{q}

for all those
q\in Q
where there exists a substitution
\delta^{\prime}
with

\psi_{x}(q)|_{\tau.\chi}=\ell\delta^{\prime}
. For these
q
,
we set
\psi_{y_{j}}(q)=\psi_{x}(q)[r_{j}\delta^{\prime}]_{\tau.\chi}
. Note that now indeed,

\psi_{x}(q)
rewrites to
\psi_{y_{j}}(q)
.
For all other
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
we set
\psi_{y_{j}}(q)=\psi_{x}(q)
.

Lemma 56

(
AC_{q}
Covers all Inner Nodes)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\cup\mathcal{P}^{(2)}
be a PTRS
with
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}\cap\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}=\varnothing
and let
\mathfrak{T}
be an
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:t)
.
Then
N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}=\bigcup_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}AC_{q}
.

Proof A.12
.

In order to show that
\bigcup_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}AC_{q}
is indeed a cover of
N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}
,
we have to show that for every node
x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}
there
exists a
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
with
x\in AC_{q}
.

In other words, we have to show that there exists at least one term
q\in Q

such that there is a substitution
\delta^{\prime}
with
\psi_{x}(q)|_{\tau.\chi}=\ell\delta^{\prime}
.
W.l.o.g., let
\operatorname{root}(\ell)\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}
.
Let
C_{1}
be the maximal context
containing no
symbols from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}
such that there exists another context
C

and terms
s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}
with

\operatorname{root}(s_{i})\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}
for all
1\leq i\leq m
such that

t_{x}=C[C_{1}[s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}]]

and the position
\pi
is
within the context
C_{1}
.

Let
\Phi
be the set of positions in
\mathrm{Pos}_{\Sigma}(t_{x})
that are within the context

C_{1}
.
There exists a term
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)

that contains all function symbols that are labeled with positions
\varphi^{-1}
where
there is a path in the origin graph from
(\mathfrak{r},\varphi^{-1})
to
(x,\varphi)

for some
\varphi\in\Phi
. This also holds if there is a normal form at position

\varphi
which may be reached from several positions
\varphi^{-1}
.
Moreover, let
\Psi=\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{m}\}
be the set of root positions
of the
s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}
within
\mathrm{Pos}(t_{x})
, i.e.,
the positions of the holes in
C_{1}
within
t_{x}
.
We can choose
q

in such a way that the fresh variables
for symbols from
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}

at positions
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
are the same whenever
there are paths in the origin graph from
(\mathfrak{r},\chi)
to
(x,\psi_{i})

and from
(\mathfrak{r},\chi^{\prime})
to
(x,\psi_{i^{\prime}})
, and
s_{i}=s_{i^{\prime}}
.

Finally, for this specific
q
we have
\psi_{x}(q)|_{\tau.\chi}=\ell\delta^{\prime}
where

\delta^{\prime}
is
like
\delta
,
but we replace every occurrence of the terms
s_{i}
with the corresponding fresh variables.

{exa}

As a final example, consider the rewrite step
(5)
, i.e.,
\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{c},\mathsf{c})\to\mathsf{c}
.
Following the notation from the proof above, we have
C_{1}=\mathsf{h}(\square,\square)
,
s_{1}=\mathsf{c}
, and
s_{2}=\mathsf{c}
.
Furthermore, we have
\psi_{1}=\chi=1
,
\psi_{2}=\chi^{\prime}=2
,
and the position
\varphi
of the
\mathsf{h}
in the context
C_{1}
is
\varphi=\varepsilon
with
\varphi^{-1}=\varepsilon
.
Hence, we choose
\mathsf{h}^{\varepsilon}(z,z)\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
,
as this term contains the function symbol
\mathsf{h}
labeled with
\varphi^{-1}=\varepsilon
,
and uses the same variable
z
for both subterms, as they are equal (
s_{1}=s_{2}=\mathsf{c}
).

See
42

Proof A.13
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\cup\mathcal{P}^{(2)}
and assume
that both
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}}
and
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}}
hold.
Let
\mathfrak{T}=(N,E,L)
be an arbitrary
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:t)
.
We prove that
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T})
is bounded by some constant
which does not depend on
\mathfrak{T}
but just on
t
.
This proves
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
.

In
Section

A.3

we defined sets
AC_{q}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{T}}
for each
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)

such that
x\in AC_{q}
if
the rewrite step at node
x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}}
is performed at some position
\pi
in
t_{x}
,

q
contains a function symbol labeled with
\tau
where there is a path from
(\mathfrak{r},\tau)
to
(x,\pi)
in the origin graph of
\mathfrak{T}
,

(x,\pi)
is labeled with some position
\chi
in the origin graph, and we can
perform the same rewrite step on
\psi_{x}(q)
at position
\tau.\chi
.
In
Lemma

56
we showed that

\bigcup_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}=N^{\mathfrak{T}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}
.

With the definition of
AC_{q}
we can now prove the upper bound on the expected derivation height of
t
.
Let
H\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
be the tree consisting of the first
H
layers of

\mathfrak{T}
.
As in the proof of the parallel execution lemma for
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle s\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}

(
Lemma

55
), for each
H\in\mathbb{N}

and each
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)

we split the tree
\mathfrak{T}_{H}
,
into (finitely many) sets
\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}

containing (finitely many) pairs of
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}}
-RSTs
and
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}
-RSTs
\mathfrak{t}
with certain probabilities
p_{\mathfrak{t}}
.
Each of these RSTs
\mathfrak{t}
starts with
(1:q)
,
has height at most
H
, and

(Prop-1)

\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}}p_{\mathfrak{t}}=1

(Prop-2)

For all
x\in AC_{q}
we have
p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}=\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}
.

(Prop-3)

For all
\mathfrak{t}\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}
,
\mathfrak{t}
starts with

(1:q)
and is a valid
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(d)}}
-RST for some
d\in\{1,2\}
.

The construction is analogous to
the ones in
54
and
55
.
Now we keep all nodes that are in
AC_{q}
and split a tree into multiple ones if
the node is not in
AC_{q}
, i.e., if the rewrite step required a different starting term in the beginning.

For example, consider the PTRS
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}
containing the rule
\mathsf{f}(x,x)\to\{1:\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c})\}
,

\mathcal{P}^{(2)}
containing the rule
\mathsf{g}(x)\to\mathsf{a}
,
and the innermost rewrite sequence
\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{b}),\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{c}))\to\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{c}))\to\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{a})\to\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c})
,
where we wrote
\to
instead of
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{3.4pt}{\tiny$\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightrightarrows}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\cup\mathcal{P}^{(2)}}
and omitted the probabilities for readability.
We split this sequence (RST) into the three sequences
\mathsf{g}(x_{1})\to\mathsf{a}
,
\mathsf{g}(x_{2})\to\mathsf{a}
, and
\mathsf{f}(x_{3},x_{3})\to\mathsf{c}
,
where all start terms are contained in
\operatorname{Abs}(\mathsf{f}(\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{b}),\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{c})))
.

Since
|\operatorname{Abs}(t)|=K
is finite
and the expected derivation lengths of all
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(d)}}
-RSTs with
d\in\{1,2\}

that start with
(1:q)
for a term
q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)
are bounded by some constant
C_{q}<\omega
,
there is a
C_{\max}<\omega
such that for all RSTs
\mathfrak{t}
with

(\mathfrak{t},p_{\mathfrak{t}})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}
we have
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{t})\leq C_{\max}
by
(Prop-3)
.
Hence, we obtain for each
H\in\mathbb{N}
:

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T}_{H})\\
&=&\sum_{x\in N^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(since $\bigcup_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}AC_{q}=N^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\setminus\operatorname{Leaf}^{\mathfrak{T}}$)}}&\leq&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\sum_{x\in AC_{q}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{T}_{H}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by \ref{itm:const-prop-2-SAST-mod})}}&=&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\sum_{x\in AC_{q}}\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(as in \eqref{eq:array-AST-parallel})}}&=&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot\sum_{x\in AC_{q}}\,p_{x}^{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by assumption)}}&\leq&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\cdot C_{\max}\\
&=&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\,C_{\max}\cdot\sum_{(p_{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathbb{T}_{H}^{q}}\,p_{\mathfrak{t}}\\
\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(by \ref{itm:const-prop-1-SAST-mod})}}&=&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\,C_{\max}\cdot 1\\
&=&\sum_{q\in\operatorname{Abs}(t)}\,C_{\max}\\
&=&K\cdot C_{\max}\end{array}

Thus, we have
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T})=\lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T}_{H})\leq K\cdot C_{\max}
.

Finally, we prove the theorem on signature extensions.

See
48

Proof A.14
.

We only prove the non-trivial direction “
\Longrightarrow
” and
consider the following three cases:

(i)

For innermost rewriting, the theorem is implied by
modularity of
\mathtt{AST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
and
\mathtt{SAST}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{i}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}

for disjoint unions
(
39
and
42
).

(ii)

For full rewriting, we first consider
the case where
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}
only contains constants and unary symbols.
Let
\Sigma^{\prime}
be another signature containing fresh symbols, i.e.,
w.l.o.g. we have

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cap\Sigma^{\prime}=\varnothing
.
For any term from
\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
we now compute
a multiset of terms from
\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{V})
which can be regarded instead.
Thus, we define a corresponding mapping
\operatorname{A}
from
\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
to
multisets of terms from
\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{V})
.
For this definition, we need two auxiliary mappings.
The mapping

\operatorname{C}:\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{V})\to\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{V})

replaces all topmost subterms with a root
f
from
\Sigma^{\prime}
by the fresh variable
x_{f}

and the mapping
\operatorname{B}
maps any term
t\in\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{V})

to the multiset that unites
\operatorname{A}(r)
for the topmost subterms
r
with
\operatorname{root}(r)\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}
occurring below a symbol from
\Sigma^{\prime}
in
t
.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\operatorname{C}(x)&=x\text{, if }x\in\mathcal{V}\\
\operatorname{C}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=f(\operatorname{C}(t_{1}),\ldots,\operatorname{C}(t_{k}))\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\\
\operatorname{C}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=x_{f}\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\prime}\\
\\
\operatorname{A}(x)&=\{x\}\text{, if }x\in\mathcal{V}\\
\operatorname{A}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\{f(\operatorname{C}(t_{1}),\ldots,\operatorname{C}(t_{k}))\}\cup\operatorname{B}(t_{1})\cup\ldots\cup\operatorname{B}(t_{k})\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\\
\operatorname{A}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\operatorname{A}(t_{1})\cup\ldots\cup\operatorname{A}(t_{k})\text{,
if }f\in\Sigma^{\prime}\\
\\
\operatorname{B}(x)&=\varnothing\text{, if }x\in\mathcal{V}\\
\operatorname{B}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\operatorname{B}(t_{1})\cup\ldots\cup\operatorname{B}(t_{k})\text{,
if }f\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\\
\operatorname{B}(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\operatorname{A}(t_{1})\cup\ldots\cup\operatorname{A}(t_{k})\text{,
if }f\in\Sigma^{\prime}\end{array}

Let
t\in\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{V})
and let
\operatorname{A}(t)=\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
.
Let
\mathsf{c}
be a fresh
n
-ary constructor symbol. Then instead of
t
, we consider the term
\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n})
.
Every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
that starts with
(1:t)
gives rise to an

\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
with
|\mathfrak{T}|=|\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}|

and
\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T})=\operatorname{edl}(\mathfrak{T}^{\prime})
.
To see this, suppose that

t\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}\{p_{1}:s_{1},\ldots,p_{k}:s_{k}\}
,

A(t)=\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
, and
A(s_{i})=\{q_{1}^{i},\ldots,q_{m_{i}}^{i}\}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
Then
there exists a
1\leq j\leq n
with
q_{j}\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}\{p_{1}:u_{1},\ldots,p_{k}:u_{k}\}
where
u_{i}\in A(s_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
For every

v_{i}\in A(s_{i})\setminus\{u_{i}\}
there exists a
j^{\prime}\neq j
with
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq n

such that
v_{i}=q_{j^{\prime}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
Note that
m_{i}\leq n
, and we might even have
m_{i}<n
if we use an erasing rule.
Hence, every step in
\mathfrak{T}
in a term
t
can be mirrored by a step in
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}

in a term
\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n})
.

Since the
q_{i}
are terms from
\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{V})
, every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
that starts with
(1:q_{i})
converges with probability 1 (resp. has
bounded expected derivation length). Hence, by the
parallel execution lemmas
(
54
and
55
) this also
holds for
every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}

that starts with
(1:\mathsf{c}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}))
(note that there cannot be any rewrite steps
at the root since
\mathsf{c}
is a constructor). But then this also holds for
every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST
\mathfrak{T}
that starts with
(1:t)
.

(iii)

Now we consider the case where

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}
contains at least one symbol
\mathsf{g}
of arity at least
2
. If
\mathsf{g}
has
an arity greater than two, then we use
the term
\mathsf{g}(\_,\,\_,\;x,...,x)
instead, where
x\in\mathcal{V}
.
Again, let
\Sigma^{\prime}
be another signature containing fresh symbols, i.e.,
w.l.o.g. we have
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cap\Sigma^{\prime}=\varnothing
.

We now define a function
\phi:\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{V})\to\mathcal{T}(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{V})

such that
\operatorname{edh}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}(t)\leq\operatorname{edh}_{\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}}(\phi(t))
:

\begin{array}[]{@{\qquad}ll}\phi(x)&=x\text{, if }x\in\mathcal{V}\\
\phi(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=f(\phi(t_{1}),\ldots,\phi(t_{k}))\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}\\
\phi(f)&=x_{f}\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ has arity 0}\\
\phi(f(t))&=\mathsf{g}(\phi(t),x_{f})\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ has arity 1}\\
\phi(f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k}))&=\mathsf{g}(\phi(t_{1}),\mathsf{g}(\phi(t_{2}),\ldots\mathsf{g}(\phi(t_{k}),x_{f})\ldots))\text{, if }f\in\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ has arity $k>1$}\end{array}

Every
\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:t)
gives rise to an

\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-RST that starts with
(1:\phi(t))

using exactly the same rules, leading to the same convergence probability and the
same expected derivation length.
To see this, note that whenever
t\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}\{p_{1}:s_{1},\ldots,p_{k}:s_{k}\}
,
then also
\phi(t)\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{2.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}\>$}}}{{\smash{\rightarrow}}}}}_{\mathcal{P}}\{p_{1}:\phi(s_{1}),\ldots,p_{k}:\phi(s_{k})\}
.
